## 🤖 Claude

I checked the repo and the link. Short version: the admin site you're asking about doesn't exist yet. The customer site is a working first version, but its look is basic.

## Is there a separate admin website?

**No.** Everything an operator does today runs from the command line, `cp-manage`: plans, subscriptions, billing events, usage and storage accounting, abuse review, nodes, capacity and restores.

That was a deliberate choice, written down at the top of `services/control_plane/manage.py`. `docs/ACCOUNTS.md` says staff access to customer data must be explicit, time-limited, audited and visible to the customer. The code doesn't have that staff model yet. Without it, an admin web UI would either make the model up or log operators in with customer accounts, so the CLI was the stopgap.

So building an admin console is an architecture decision (an ADR), not just frontend work. At minimum it needs:
- **Staff logins kept separate from customer accounts,** with MFA required.
- **Served only on the internal listener,** never the public app (ADR-037).
- **Read-only to start:** sales, subscriptions, billing events, usage per project, node capacity, abuse queue. Those numbers already exist behind `cp-manage`, so the first version is mostly putting them on a page.
- **Every staff look at a customer organization recorded in `audit_events`** as staff, not customer.

## The customer site as it stands

`frontend/` is plain HTML, CSS and JS with no build step, about 5k lines. It has the landing page, pricing, sign-up and sign-in, organizations and projects, plan and usage with Stripe upgrade, and memory spaces. Its own README says what's missing: **API key management, the usage and billing pages, and the direct-connection panel.** It's one long scrolling page, not a real app.

## The template

That link is **Duralux**, a paid Bootstrap admin-dashboard template. The fetch only returned the store's wrapper page, not the demo itself, so I haven't seen its actual pages. Two things follow:
- **Its style fits the logged-in console and an admin console,** not the marketing page. Admin templates have a sidebar, KPI cards, tables and charts, which suits screens like projects, API keys, usage and billing. A landing page styled like that looks like a back office.
- **We should take ideas from it, not its code.** It's licensed, and it's jQuery/Bootstrap, which would break the no-dependencies rule the frontend was built on. I'd rebuild the look (layout, spacing, card and table patterns) in our own CSS.

## Suggested order

1. **Console layout:** a sidebar app shell, with separate screens for Overview, Projects, a project's page (API keys, usage, database, memory), Billing and Settings. This also fills the three missing panels.
2. **Tidy the landing and sign-in pages** so they look like the same product without looking like a dashboard.
3. **Admin console:** the ADR first, then read-only screens on the internal app, reusing the same visual design.

If you can save a few screenshots of the Duralux demo pages into the repo, I'll match the look more closely than I can from its name.

## 🤖 Claude

I redesigned the customer site and opened PR #192. I haven't merged it; that's waiting on you.

**Is there a separate admin website?** No. Everything an operator does (sales, subscriptions, billing events, usage, capacity, abuse review, restores) is a `cp-manage` command. That was deliberate: `docs/ACCOUNTS.md` says staff access to a customer's organization must be explicit, time-limited, audited and visible to the customer, and the code has no model for that yet. An admin site needs that model and an ADR before anything gets built. I wrote up the open questions in `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` under "Operator web console".

**What changed on the customer site** (the look follows Duralux's layout; no code taken from it, and still no dependencies or build step):
- **Signed in:** a left sidebar (a slide-out menu on phones), a page header with title and breadcrumb, and a project list shown as a table with count cards above it.
- **Every project feature has its own page:** overview, SQL editor, tables, API keys, plan & usage, and memory. API keys, usage and memory used to be buttons that opened inside each project's card.
  - The new overview shows the project's status, API URL, publishable key with copy buttons, usage cards and plan.
- **Organization and access tokens** pages are laid out in cards.
- **Public pages:** the landing page, pricing and sign-in card are restyled to match.
- **Light and dark themes:** the system setting by default, with a toggle in the header.
- **Unchanged:** the API and how the site talks to it.

**Checks:**
- All 62 frontend tests pass and the linter is clean.
- **Browser check:** I opened every page in headless Chrome against the dev control plane, at desktop width in light and dark and at phone width. There were no page errors and no sideways scrolling.
- **Secret keys:**
  - A new secret key disappears when you leave the API keys page; before, it disappeared when you closed its panel. I created a test key to confirm this and revoked it afterwards.
  - I updated the API keys test to check this.
- **New test** for the project pages: it checks that every value put into the page is escaped, including values nested inside lists.
  - The older panel tests can't see those nested values. I ran the deeper check over the old code by hand and found nothing unescaped.

The security review found nothing, and it's recorded on the commit.

**To look at it:** the redesign is at `http://127.0.0.1:5174` on the dev box (the demo login in `.dev/dashboard-demo-login` works). The copy you saw before, on `10.120.0.165:5173`, is still the old version and is running from a worktree that has since been deleted. Say the word and I'll point that address at the new one.

The next step would be the admin console, starting with the ADR.

## 🤖 Claude

Yes, it can go on the `.173` box, but I didn't deploy it: Claude Code's permission check blocked the change on the VM as a production deploy. I've left that decision to you rather than find a way around it.

**What I found (read-only):**
- `10.120.0.173` serves the website at `https://test.maludb.org` straight out of the git checkout at `/opt/maludb/frontend`.
- That checkout is clean, on `main` at `5aebc00`. The PR branch starts from that same commit.
- The PR only changes the frontend, docs and tests, so switching the checkout to the PR branch updates the website without touching the running control plane. No service restart is needed.

**To deploy it**, run this yourself (the `!` prefix runs it in this session):

```
! ssh -i ~/.ssh/maludb_rehearsal maludb@10.120.0.173 'cd /opt/maludb && sudo git fetch -q origin feat/console-redesign && sudo git checkout -q -B feat/console-redesign FETCH_HEAD && sudo git log --oneline -1 && sudo git status --short'
```

Two things to know:
- **The VM will be on an unmerged branch.** Once #192 merges, switch it back with `sudo git checkout main && sudo git pull`.
- **To undo:** `sudo git checkout main`.

Signups stay closed on that site, because the "signups open" switch in `index.html` is still `false`.

If you'd rather I run it, allow it (approve the prompt, or add a permission rule for ssh to that host). I'll then deploy it and sign in on `https://test.maludb.org` in a headless browser to check it.

## 🤖 Claude

I wrote ADR-082 and opened it as PR #193 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/193). I haven't merged it. You picked my recommendation on all four questions, so I marked the ADR **Accepted** with your choices written into it; tell me if you meant them only as input to a draft.

**What ADR-082 decides:**
- **Staff aren't customers.** Staff accounts are completely separate from customer accounts: their own records, sign-in and sessions. Signing in takes a password plus an authenticator-app code, every time. Accounts are created and revoked only with `cp-manage staff …` commands.
- **A private, separate site.** The console is a third application on its own port, bound to a private address and reached over the VPN. It isn't added to the existing internal listener, which handles traffic from the nodes.
- **No platform secrets in it.** The console process never loads the main encryption key and has no node credentials.
  - Staff authenticator codes are protected by a separate key that only protects those codes.
  - It connects to the database with a restricted login that cannot read any encrypted or password columns.
  - Tests will enforce both.
- **Read-only first.** The reports come from what `cp-manage` already computes: subscriptions and billing events, usage against plan limits, the abuse report, node capacity, and failed provisioning jobs. Actions like suspending or moving a project stay in `cp-manage`. Adding any of them to the console needs an amendment to the ADR.
- **Business records vs customer data.**
  - **Visible to staff:** org names, owner emails, plans, usage figures and billing events. Each look at one organization is logged internally; customers don't see those entries.
  - **Not reachable at all:** anything inside a customer's database, and any keys. Reaching that later would be "support access", which needs its own ADR and is visible to the customer.
- **Money figures come from Stripe**, not from sums the platform computes. There's one staff role for now.

**What it costs:**
- A second encryption key to keep and back up.
- A new service to deploy.
- A VPN into the control-plane host.
- Whoever holds the staff key can decrypt staff authenticator secrets; they would still need each staff member's password. If that key is lost, every staff member re-enrols with `cp-manage`.

The PR also adds `plans/active/operator-console.md`, which breaks the work into six slices: staff accounts and sign-in first, then the admin site and its port, the restricted database login, the reports, the web pages, and deployment. It also closes the "Operator web console" entry in `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`.

The next step is slice 0, staff accounts and sign-in. Say when you want it started, or merge #193 first.

## 🤖 Claude

I merged #193 (the ADR, `9db4dad`) and built slice 0, staff accounts with sign-in, as PR #194 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/194). I haven't merged #194.

**What slice 0 adds** (no web pages or HTTP routes yet; those come in slice 1):
- **Separate staff accounts:** three new tables for staff accounts, their authenticator codes and their sessions, with no link to or from customer accounts.
- **Sign-in:** needs the password and the 6-digit authenticator code together.
  - **Codes can't be reused.** Each code works only once, even if two sign-in attempts race with the same one.
  - **Lockout:** five failed attempts lock the account for 15 minutes.
  - **Sessions** last 8 hours, end after 30 minutes idle, and end when the account is revoked, re-enrolled or its password is changed.
- **Credentials can't be swapped:** a staff login token is rejected everywhere a customer's is accepted, and customer tokens are rejected on the staff side. Tests cover both directions.
- **The staff key:** authenticator secrets are encrypted under this key, set by `MALUDB_STAFF_KEY_REF`. The code refuses to use the platform's main encryption key for this.
- **Commands:** `cp-manage staff create | enrol | password | revoke | list`.
  - Creating an account prints the authenticator secret once and doesn't finish until you type in a working code.
  - Every account change and sign-in is written to the audit log as a staff action.

**Security review found one real problem, now fixed.** The gateway's database login gets access to every control-plane table except a short exclusion list, and the gateway is the internet-facing process on the node. The new staff tables weren't on that list. After a routine re-grant, a compromised gateway could have written itself a staff session and had operator access.
- I added the staff tables to the exclusion list.
- The deployment readiness check (`cp-manage deploy preflight`) now flags any access at all to those tables, not just reading.
- The new gateway test fails when the fix is removed.

The same trap applies to any future table holding secrets or identity.

**Checks:**
- 26 new tests, including the official test codes from the TOTP standard (RFC 6238) and an end-to-end run of the `cp-manage staff` commands.
- 138 tests pass across the staff, gateway, readiness-check, identity and log-redaction suites.
- Lint is clean, the OpenAPI spec is unchanged, and re-running migrations does nothing.

I also updated `docs/SECRETS.md` (what the staff key is and what happens if it's lost or leaked), `docs/ACCOUNTS.md`, the schema spec and the plan.

Next is slice 1: the admin application on its own private port, with staff sign-in and sign-out routes.

## 🤖 Claude

I haven't merged #194 yet: its main CI check is still running, and its other check (the security-review gate) has passed. Slice 1 is built, tested and committed on a local branch that sits on top of slice 0, but I haven't opened its PR yet.

**What slice 1 adds** (the operator console's own application):
- **A third application** that only platform staff reach. It has a health check and sign-in routes under `/admin/v1/session`: sign in with email, password and code; see who you are; sign out. The customer-facing and internal applications don't include any of these routes.
- **One secret.** The console's config has no main encryption key and no platform pepper, only the staff key. Staff session tokens now take their pepper from the staff key, so it's the only secret the console holds.
- **Browser session:** the session is a cookie that page scripts can't read, other sites can't trigger, and is only sent over HTTPS, scoped to `/admin`. Any request that changes state also needs an `X-MaluDB-Staff` header, which a form on another site can't send.
  - Every failed sign-in gets the same 401 response; the actual reason goes to the audit log.
  - Sign-in attempts are rate-limited per client.
- **Deployment files:**
  - A systemd unit running under its own user and environment file, given only the staff key, on port 8113 at the address in `MALUDB_ADMIN_BIND`.
  - An example environment file with no main key and no pepper.
- **Readiness check:** `cp-manage deploy preflight` now fails if the console binds a wildcard or public address, or if the staff key is the same material as the main encryption key.
- **Tests:** 210 pass across the new admin-app tests and the staff, surface, identity, gateway, unit-file and readiness-check suites. The OpenAPI spec is unchanged, and my security review found nothing.

It isn't deployable yet. The console is meant to connect with its own restricted database login, which slice 2 creates, and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` §1.7 says to wait for that.

## 🤖 Claude

#195 is still in CI; the background watcher will tell me when it finishes. Where things stand:

- **#195 (slice 1, the console's application):** waiting on CI. You authorised merging it, and I will as soon as it's green.
- **Slice 2 (the console's own database login):** built, tested and committed locally, on top of slice 1. I'll open its PR once #195 is merged and slice 2 is rebased onto `main`.

**What slice 2 does:**
- **Its own database login.** The console connects as a member of a new `cp_admin_console` group instead of the control plane's own login. Access is an explicit list of columns: what staff sign-in and sessions use, plus two non-secret ID columns (`nodes`, `projects`) that an existing row-security rule on `audit_events` reads.
- **Explicitly refused, and checked against the database's permissions:**
  - reading any encrypted column (node, project, API key, email, customer MFA and provider-key secrets, the wrapped data keys);
  - reading any customer password hash or token hash;
  - creating a staff account, or changing a staff password, status, email or authenticator secret;
  - deleting sessions or audit records;
  - being a superuser, or able to bypass row security.
- **A migration that lets the console write only staff audit events.** Without it, the console couldn't record a sign-in: the only existing rule on `audit_events` allows the gateway's own-node rows.
- **`cp-manage admin-console grant`** applies the permissions. It refuses if the group also contains a gateway, health reporter or memory worker login.
- **Refuses a wider login.** The console won't start in production under a login with more access than this, or one that isn't in the group. The readiness check (`preflight`) reports the same problems, and flags when a new migration means the grant must be re-run.

**Testing:** the new test runs the whole sign-in flow in production mode as the restricted login: a refused sign-in, a successful one, who-am-I, sign-out. It also confirms the database refuses each forbidden read and write. 197 tests pass across the related suites.

**Security review found one real issue, now fixed.** The console's startup check for "is this login also a gateway?" read a table whose row security hides every row from the console. So it could only ever answer "no".
- **Now:** at startup the console checks only what it can see, which is its own group memberships. The gateway and health-reporter overlap is checked by the grant command and the readiness check, which run with the control plane's login.
- **Also confirmed:** removing the two ID-column grants breaks audit writes, so they stay.

`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` §1.7 now has the full setup, including creating the database login. The console is deployable, though it can only sign staff in and out until the reports arrive in slice 3.

## 🤖 Claude

I merged #196, the console's own database login, into `main` (`c5c0628`). The first set of reports is up as PR #197: https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/197. I haven't merged #197, and CI has just started on it.

Slice 3 was too big for one PR, so I split it into three:
- **3a, sales and customers:** this PR.
- **3b, usage and abuse:** storage, file storage, egress and email per project against plan limits, and the abuse report.
- **3c, nodes and provisioning:** capacity and health per node, and projects that failed or are waiting to retry.

**What 3a adds** (read-only, staff sign-in required):

| Page | Shows |
|---|---|
| Overview | organizations, users and new users this week, projects serving and failed, projects by plan, subscriptions by state, failed payments still inside the grace period, paid-for plan changes the maintenance pass hasn't applied yet, billing events that failed this week |
| Sales | every subscription (filter by state), the grace-period countdown, plan changes still waiting to be applied |
| Billing events | what Stripe sent and what happened to each |
| Customers | organizations, newest first, searchable by name or member email |
| One customer | members, projects with usage figures, subscriptions, that customer's billing events. Opening it writes a `staff.view` audit entry |

**How it's kept safe:**
- **No billing or provisioning code.** The reports use their own queries, which import only the database helper, rather than the existing billing and subscription modules. Those modules pull in the Stripe client and provisioning code, and the import test now fails if the console reaches them.
- **Read-only row access.** A migration lets the console read rows in `projects`, `subscriptions` and `billing_events`, which have row-level security. It can read, not write.
- **Only the columns the reports read.** The console's database access grows by exactly those columns; customer password hashes and every encrypted column stay off-limits.
- **No secrets or payment amounts.** Every response lists its fields explicitly, and none of them is a secret or a money figure.

**Testing:**
- The new test seeds two customers, including a past-due subscription and a failed billing event. It then calls every report as the console's restricted database login in production mode. It checks the contents, that there are no secrets in any response, that every report needs sign-in, and that only opening one customer writes `staff.view`.
- Removing one needed column from the console's database permissions makes the test fail with `permission denied`, which confirms it really runs as that login.
- 125 tests pass across the console, grants, readiness-check and route-surface suites, and my security review found nothing.

Say the word to merge #197 once it's green and start 3b.

## 🤖 Claude

Where things stand:

- **#197 (slice 3a, sales and customer reports):** CI's main `check` job is still running. It will be merged as soon as it passes.
- **3b (usage and abuse reports):** built, tested and committed on a branch stacked on 3a. Once #197 is merged I'll rebase 3b onto `main`, re-run its tests and open its PR.

**What 3b adds** (read-only, staff sign-in required):
- **`/admin/v1/usage`** lists every live project against its plan's limits:
  - database and file storage (stored size and state from the maintenance pass);
  - egress this month;
  - emails sent in the last day.

  Highest-pressure projects come first, and you can filter by plan.
- **`/admin/v1/abuse`** is the same data as `cp-manage abuse report`, as a page. It covers one plan (free by default), breaks ties toward the youngest accounts, and has an optional minimum percentage. It reports only; it never suspends anything.

**Details:**
- Each meter shows used, limit, percent, state and when it was measured.
- A project that has never been measured shows no figure rather than a misleading zero.
- A project using something its plan allows zero of is flagged `over_zero_ceiling` instead of showing an infinite percentage.
- A migration lets the console read egress and email-event rows. It can't read the hashed recipient-address column, and that column is now on the forbidden list the readiness check verifies.

**Testing:** 161 tests pass across the console suites, the database-access checks for the gateway and memory worker, the readiness check, and the existing customer usage and abuse-control tests. The new tests run both reports as the console's restricted database login against seeded data: one project over its database limit, one at 90% egress, and an unmeasured project. Those checks confirm the order, percentages, states, plan filter and the zero-limit flag.

## 🤖 Claude

3b isn't merged yet: its PR, #198, has just started CI. I'll merge it when it goes green, as you asked. 3c is built, tested and committed on a local branch on top of 3b.

- **#197 (3a, sales and customers):** merged into `main` as `686e61b`.
- **#198 (3b, usage and abuse):** rebased onto #197's merge with no conflicts. The 44 console tests (reports, app, grants) pass after the rebase.
- **3c (nodes and provisioning):** once #198 is merged, I'll rebase 3c onto `main` and open its PR.

**What 3c adds** (read-only, staff sign-in required):
- **`/admin/v1/nodes`**, per node:
  - health, flagged stale after 5 minutes;
  - projects and running projects against their limits;
  - projected database connections, replication slots and free disk;
  - Realtime and backup readiness;
  - whether placement would accept a new project, and why not if it wouldn't.
- **`/admin/v1/provisioning`:** projects that failed or are waiting to retry, with the last job's attempt and error code, plus projects stuck in setup for more than 15 minutes. It only reports; `cp-manage project retry` is still how you act on them.

**One refactor to review.** The capacity logic lived in `nodes`, the module that also decrypts node superuser credentials, which the console must never import. I moved the capacity code into its own `node_capacity` module. `nodes` re-exports every name from it, so placement code is unchanged, and the placement, maintenance, Realtime, tenant-move and project-creation tests all still pass.

**Kept off the console:** node hostnames, internal addresses, and the free-text error detail on provisioning jobs, which can quote a node's own error messages. They're on the console's forbidden-column list, and the test checks that neither the hostname nor the internal address appears in any response.

**Testing:** 216 tests pass across the console, nodes, maintenance, grants, readiness-check, route-surface and extension-pin suites. Another 68 pass across the Realtime, tenant-move and project-creation suites. 17 Realtime tests skipped because they need a Realtime-prepared database cluster this box doesn't have.

## 🤖 Claude

I merged 3b, and 3c is up as PR #199 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/199), now in CI. I'll merge it when green, as you asked. Slice 4, the console's web pages, is built, tested in a browser and committed locally on top of 3c.

- **#198 (3b, usage and abuse):** merged into `main` as `4371fb1`.
- **#199 (3c, nodes and provisioning):** rebased onto `main`. 87 tests pass across the console suites and nodes, and lint is clean.
- **Slice 4:** rebased onto 3c. Once #199 is merged I'll rebase it onto `main` and open its PR.

**What slice 4 adds:**
- **Pages under `/admin/` on the private listener:** staff sign-in (email, password, authenticator code), overview, sales, billing events, customers and a single customer, usage, abuse review, nodes and provisioning.
  - Filters are kept in the address, so a filtered page can be reloaded or sent to other staff.
  - It reuses the customer console's stylesheet, so both consoles look alike, with light and dark themes.
- **Only five named files are served.** There's no directory mount, so a crafted path can't reach any other file; the tests confirm path-traversal attempts get a 404. The customer-facing and internal apps serve none of these pages.
- **Strict browser policy.** Scripts and styles load only from files on the same origin, with no inline code, and the pages can't be framed. The browser keeps nothing except the theme. The session cookie is invisible to the page's script, and every sign-in or sign-out sends the anti-forgery header.

**Checks:**
- 17 static tests: escaping at every nesting depth (a deliberately unescaped value was caught), browser storage, the header, that every API path the page calls exists, no inline script or style, and exactly which files are served.
- **Headless Chrome against seeded demo data**, at desktop width in light and dark and at phone width:
  - every page loaded, with no page errors, no content-policy violations and no sideways scrolling;
  - a `<script>` I planted in a billing event's note displayed as plain text;
  - signing out cleared the page.
- **One flaky moment was my test script, not the console:** a second sign-in reused an authenticator code, and the replay guard correctly refused it. The script now waits for a fresh code.

**If you want to click through it yourself:** it's running now on this box at `http://127.0.0.1:8113/admin/`, against the test database with demo data. Sign in as `ops@maludb.test` with password `a-long-staff-password-demo`. The authenticator secret to add to your app is in `/home/maludb/ui-work/admin/totp-secret`.

Once #199 merges I'll open the slice 4 PR. After that, only slice 5 remains: deploying the console to the rehearsal control plane behind the VPN.

CI is running again on the fixed commit, which will take about 20 minutes; the watcher will notify me when it's done.

**The first run failed on one test from slice 0, not on slice 4's code.** `test_staff_identity.py` pinned "now" to 12:00 UTC today, while sessions are also checked against the database's real clock. Eight hours later (the session lifetime), the staff listing found no live session, so the test began failing at 20:00 UTC. `main` has the same test, so merging #200 fixes it there too. The fix is its own commit: the tests use the current minute, and all 26 pass after 20:00 UTC.

**Already done on `.173`** (the steps that don't need slice 4's code):
- **Staff key:** `/etc/maludb/keys/staff-key` generated, root-only, and confirmed different from the KEK.
- **Service user:** `maludb-admin` created.
- **Database roles:** a `cp_admin_console` group and a `cp_admin` login in it; neither is a superuser. The password was generated on the box and passed to `psql` on standard input, never on a command line or through this chat.
- **Environment file:** `/etc/maludb/admin-console.env`, root-only:
  - production mode and the `cp_admin` database connection;
  - bound to `10.120.0.173`;
  - the Secure cookie flag off, since this listener is plain HTTP on your VPN-only LAN.

  It holds no KEK and no pepper.

When CI is green I'll merge #200, then on `.173`:
1. Pull `main` and apply migrations 0051–0055, then restart the public, internal and provisioner services.
2. Run `cp-manage admin-console grant`, and re-run the gateway grant so the new staff tables are explicitly blocked for the gateway.
3. Install and start the console unit.
4. Check it:
   - it answers from this box;
   - `test.maludb.org/admin/` doesn't serve it;
   - the readiness check passes.

## 🤖 Claude

The operator console is deployed on the rehearsal control plane at **http://10.120.0.173:8113/admin/**, reachable only on your private network. Slice 4 is merged. There's no staff account yet; that's yours to create (command below).

**Before deploying:**
- **Slice 4 merged** as #200 (`5752f00`), together with a fix to a slice-0 test that had started failing at 20:00 UTC. It pinned "now" to noon today, so its 8-hour session had expired by the real clock. `main` had the same bug, and the merge fixed it there too.

**What changed on `.173`:**
- **Code:** updated from this morning's commit to current `main`, migrations 0051–0055 applied, and the public, internal and provisioner services restarted. All three report healthy, and `test.maludb.org` still works.
- **Secrets:** a staff key generated on the box (root-only, confirmed different from the main encryption key). The console's restricted database login `cp_admin` was created with a password generated on the box, stored only in the root-only `/etc/maludb/admin-console.env`.
- **Grants:** the console's database permissions applied, and the gateway's re-applied; the gateway login has no access to any staff table.
- **The console service** runs as its own `maludb-admin` user, on `10.120.0.173:8113` only.
  - It started in production mode as the restricted login, so its startup check (which refuses a login with more access) passed.
  - The Secure cookie flag is off because this listener is plain HTTP on the LAN behind your VPN.

**Verified:**
- **From this box:** the health check answers and the console pages carry the security headers. The reports refuse requests without sign-in, and path-traversal attempts get 404.
- **In a real browser:** the live sign-in page loads with no errors, and a wrong login gets the standard refusal.
- **Not reachable publicly:** `https://test.maludb.org/admin/`, its script, and `/api/admin/v1/...` all return 404, and the public IP doesn't answer on port 8113.
- **Readiness check:** the console's two checks pass, one for the private bind address and separate staff key, one for the restricted database login. The run still reports two failures that were there before today and are unrelated: no signup challenge required, and no scheduled maintenance pass.

**Create your staff account** from a machine that can reach `.173`. It asks for a password twice, prints the authenticator secret once, then waits for a code from your app:
```
ssh -t -i ~/.ssh/maludb_rehearsal maludb@10.120.0.173 "sudo bash -c 'cd /opt/maludb && set -a && . /etc/maludb/control-plane.env && set +a && MALUDB_STAFF_KEY_REF=/etc/maludb/keys/staff-key /opt/maludb/.venv/bin/python -m services.control_plane.manage staff create --email edward.honour@gmail.com --name Edward'"
```
Then sign in at http://10.120.0.173:8113/admin/.

**Docs:** the deployment turned up three corrections to the runbook in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` §1.7, and I've folded them in:
- `staff create` needs `control-plane.env` loaded and a real terminal;
- re-run the gateway grant after the staff-table migrations;
- the readiness check needs the console's two settings in its environment.

They're in docs PR #201 with the rehearsal log (not merged). The node `.172` wasn't touched.

One loose end: the local demo console on `127.0.0.1:8113`, pointed at the test database, is probably still running on this box. I can stop it if you don't need it.

I saved the command as **`/home/maludb/create-staff-account.sh`** on this box, made it executable, and syntax-checked it. I haven't run it.

Run it from a real terminal, because it prompts you:
```
~/create-staff-account.sh
```
It uses `edward.honour@gmail.com` and the name Edward unless you pass others: `~/create-staff-account.sh you@example.com "Your Name"`.

1. It asks for a password twice, at least 16 characters.
2. It prints the authenticator secret and an `otpauth://` link once. Add that to your authenticator app straight away.
3. It asks for the 6-digit code your app shows. You get three tries; if all fail, re-enrol with `staff enrol`.

Then sign in at http://10.120.0.173:8113/admin/ from your private network or VPN.

It connects with the `~/.ssh/maludb_rehearsal` key on this box, so run it here, or copy the key along with the script.

## 🤖 Claude

The maintenance pass is the control plane's recurring housekeeping job, `cp-manage maintenance run` (code in `services/control_plane/maintenance.py`). It's built to be run about once a minute by a systemd timer or cron job, not as a long-running service, so an operator can see it, run it by hand, or stop it. Every step is safe to interrupt or run twice; a run that dies halfway just picks up the rest next time.

**What one run does:**

| Step | What it does |
|---|---|
| Retry | Retries projects whose provisioning failed and whose retry time has come |
| Grace | Ends the grace period for failed payments that have run out (ADR-051) |
| Billing | Applies what customers paid for. The Stripe webhook only records a purchase; this step actually moves the project to the new plan (ADR-053) |
| Storage | Measures each project's database size and sets its storage state, including restricting writes when it's over the limit |
| Object storage | Measures file storage and reconciles objects with the object store |
| Slots, capacity, backups | Checks replication slots, node capacity and backup readiness |
| Plan drift | Reports projects whose plan differs from what's paid for |
| Sleep | Stops each project's PostgREST and Auth workers after 15 minutes idle |

**Why the readiness check fails without it:**
- **Paid plans don't apply.** A customer can pay and nothing changes.
- **Usage figures stay empty.** That's why the console shows "never measured" everywhere.
- **Storage limits aren't enforced**, and failed payments never reach the point where writes are restricted.
- **Idle workers never sleep.** ADR-022 says the free tier's economics depend on that.

**Why it isn't scheduled on the rehearsal yet: it's an open question where it should run** (`docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`, "Where the maintenance pass runs").
- **Control-plane work:** most of the job needs the encryption key and every node's admin credentials. Those belong on the control plane (`.173`), not on a node.
- **Node work:** the step that sleeps idle workers stops systemd units, and those run on the node (`.172`).

No single host in the two-machine setup has both. The options are:
1. Run it on the control plane and have the sleep step reach the node remotely.
2. Split it into a control-plane job and a node job, each with its own timer.
3. Run it on the node with access to the control-plane database.

That's a decision for you before launch.

## 🧑 User

what makes the most sense?

## 🤖 Claude

**Option 2: split it.** Most of the job runs on the control plane, and the step that sleeps idle workers runs on each node, as the gateway. The deciding fact is that the gateway already holds every permission the sleep step needs, and no other host does.

**The node half needs no new privileges.** Sleeping a worker takes three things, and the gateway already has all three:
- **Read activity.** The gateway writes `worker_last_active_at` on every request, and its database login can already read and update that column.
- **Update worker state.** It already sets `worker_state` when it wakes a worker; sleeping just sets it back.
- **Stop the systemd units.** `deploy/50-maludb-gateway.rules` already lets the `maludb-gateway` user start, stop and restart exactly `maludb-(postgrest|gotrue|realtime)@<ref>.service`, and nothing else.

A node timer that runs as `maludb-gateway` with the gateway's own database login therefore adds no new credential. It also scopes itself automatically: that login's row rules only show projects on its own node, which is also what makes this work when there's a second node.

**The control-plane half stays where its secrets already are.** Grace expiry, billing, storage measurement, capacity and backup checks, and provisioning retries need the KEK and node admin credentials. That access already lives on `.173` under ADR-038, so a timer running `cp-manage maintenance run` there just skips the sleep step.

**Why not the other two options:**
- **Option 1, the control plane reaching into the node:** it needs a standing route for the control plane to run commands on nodes, over SSH or a remote systemd API. That's a new, broad trust path from the host that holds every node's superuser password.
- **Option 3, running everything on the node:** it puts the KEK and every node's admin credentials on an internet-facing host. That breaks ADR-038 and ADR-072, and a compromised node could open every other node's database.

**What it would take** (a small ADR plus one PR):
1. **ADR:** record the split and close the open question.
2. **Sleep command on the node:** a node-side command that runs only the sleep step, as the gateway's login, plus a `maludb-node-maintenance.timer`.
   - The existing query that finds idle workers isn't filtered by node. That's harmless as the gateway, whose row rules hide other nodes, but I'd add the filter explicitly.
3. **Control-plane job without sleep:** `cp-manage maintenance run --skip sleep` (or split the passes cleanly), plus a `maludb-maintenance.timer` on the control plane.
4. **Readiness check:** check both halves, not just "has it ever run".
5. **Rehearsal:** install both timers on `.172` and `.173`. That also clears one of the two readiness failures.

Want me to draft the ADR?

🔧 **Bash**: `cat /tmp/claude-1000/-home-maludb-maludb-provisioner-api/cbf9c726-4f37-4934-965b-e9ba3223b2b8/tasks/bpooys6sy.output; cd`

## 🤖 Claude

I wrote ADR-083 as **Proposed** and opened it as PR #203 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/203). `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` now points to it. Nothing is built or merged; it needs your acceptance first.

**What it proposes:**
1. **Two timers.**
   - On the control plane: every maintenance step except sleeping idle workers, run as the control plane's own database login.
   - On each node: only the sleep step, run as the gateway's OS user with the gateway's restricted database login.
2. **No new credentials on the node.** The gateway login can already read and update its own node's worker state, and the existing permission rule already lets the gateway user start and stop exactly the per-project services. The node job never loads the encryption key or any node admin credential, and a test will enforce that.
3. **The control-plane job stops trying to sleep workers**, which fails there anyway because those services only exist on the node.
4. **The idle-worker queries filter by node explicitly**, instead of relying only on row-level security.
5. **Each half records its runs where only it can write:**
   - the existing `maintenance_runs` table becomes off-limits to the gateway;
   - a new `node_maintenance_runs` table restricts each gateway to rows for its own node.
6. **The readiness check verifies both.** It fails if an active node has no completed sleep run in the last 10 minutes.

**A security gap found while drafting.** `maintenance_runs` has no row-level security and isn't on the gateway's exclusion list. So a compromised gateway could insert a fake "finished" run, and the readiness check would report a stopped billing and storage job as healthy. Point 5 closes this. It's the same kind of gap the staff tables had in slice 0.

**Rejected alternatives:**
- **The control plane reaching into each node:** a standing command path from the host that holds every node's superuser password.
- **Everything on the node:** puts the encryption key and all admin credentials on the internet-facing host.
- **A loop inside the gateway process:** kept as a fallback, since the ADR prefers separately run jobs an operator can see and stop.

**If you accept it, the build:**
- one migration;
- an option to run only some maintenance steps, plus a node-side sleep command;
- two timers;
- the readiness-check changes;
- re-running the gateway permission grant on every node;
- installing both timers on `.172` and `.173`, which clears one of the two readiness-check failures.

**PR #202** is still in CI and will merge as soon as it's green.

## 🤖 Claude

I wrote the free-tier launch plan and opened it as PR #204 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/204). Nothing is built yet. It opens the Developer tier as a public beta on the current VMs at test.maludb.org, based on your answers.

**What's missing today** (from checking both VMs):
- **Maintenance pass:** nothing schedules it. Free projects are never measured, never restricted at their storage limit, and idle workers never sleep.
- **Email:** MaluMail isn't configured, so platform password resets and end-user Auth emails send nothing.
- **Auth:** GoTrue is installed on the node but has never run for a real signup.
- **Storage:** no object store or storage service on the node, and `DEPLOYMENT.md` has no section for setting them up.
- **Memory spaces:** the memory worker, embedder, egress proxy and their database logins aren't set up.
- **Backups:** pgBackRest is installed but WAL archiving is off, and there's no off-site copy.
- **Rate limits:** requests arrive through the reverse proxy from one address, so signup and sign-in limits treat everyone as one client. The captcha secret isn't set either.
- **Leftovers:** the previous install's services are still on the VMs (disabled), along with an old database access line.

**The plan, in order:**
1. **Maintenance pass:** build ADR-083 and install both timers.
2. **Email:** MaluMail for password resets and Auth mail, verified end to end.
3. **Auth:** end-user sign-up, email confirmation and sign-in, checked with the official Supabase client.
4. **Storage:** object store and storage service on the node, a `DEPLOYMENT.md` section for it, and a check that one project can't read another's files.
5. **Memory spaces:** worker, embedder, egress proxy and their database logins; ingest and search tested with real model keys.
6. **Vector search and schema graph:** confirmed working on a free project.
7. **Backups:** pgBackRest on the node, an off-site repository, a scheduled control-plane backup, and a test restore of one project.
8. **Abuse controls:** rate limits that see real client addresses, plus the Turnstile captcha.
9. **Public site:** Developer shown as available and paid tiers as "coming", with legal and support links.
10. **Launch check:** remove the leftovers, get a clean readiness check, run a stranger's signup end to end, and only then open signups.

**What I need from you along the way:**
- **H-1:** put the MaluMail key in a root-only file on `.173` (not in chat) and tell me the sending address.
- **H-2:** Cloudflare Turnstile site key and secret.
- **H-3:** an off-site backup target, and where a copy of the KEK will be kept.
- **H-4:** terms of service, privacy policy and acceptable-use policy text.
- **H-5:** who reviews the abuse report, and how often.
- **H-6:** a support address and where incidents get announced.

**PRs:**

- **#203** (ADR-083, marked accepted): open. Slice 1 builds on it, so it's best merged first. Can I merge it once its CI passes?
- **#204** (this launch plan): open, docs only.

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 1 (the maintenance-pass split) is built and committed locally, but has no PR yet. #202 is merged. #203 (ADR-083) is still in CI and will be merged automatically when it passes. Then I'll rebase slice 1 onto `main` and open its PR.

**What slice 1 does:**
- **Control plane:**
  - `cp-manage maintenance run` accepts `--skip`, and a skipped step is never called.
  - A new timer runs `maintenance run --skip sleep` every minute, as the provisioner user with its environment and keys.
- **Node:**
  - A new `node_maintenance` job, run every minute by its own timer, sleeps this node's idle PostgREST, GoTrue and Realtime workers.
  - It runs as `maludb-gateway` with the gateway's database login, has no KEK, and its queries name the node explicitly.
- **Supporting refactor:** the small systemd supervisor class moved to its own `supervision` module, so the node job doesn't import `workers`, which pulls in provisioning code. `workers` re-exports it, so nothing else changes.
- **Run records:**
  - A migration adds `node_maintenance_runs`; each gateway can only write and read its own node's rows.
  - `maintenance_runs` is now blocked for the gateway, closing the fake "healthy" run hole.
  - The readiness check gains a "node maintenance" check.
- **Docs:** `DEPLOYMENT.md` has a control-plane timer section (§1.5a) and a node section (§2.6), including the reminder to re-run the gateway grant after upgrading.

**Testing:**
- **The node job runs as a real gateway login against two nodes.** It sleeps only its own node's idle workers, leaves recently used ones and ones it failed to stop running, and records runs only for its own node. It can't read or write the control-plane run table, and a login that isn't any node's gateway sleeps nothing.
- **Also covered:** the skip option, the new service files, and the readiness check.
- **Result:** 360 tests pass across the maintenance, workers, gateway, grants, readiness-check, route-surface and memory-worker suites. The one failure along the way was expected: a readiness-check fixture now also has to record a node maintenance run, and it passes with that added.

The security review found nothing. The change removes a way to fake a healthy run and adds no new access.

After the PR is green and merged, slice 1 finishes with deployment:
1. Pull `main` on both VMs, apply migration 0056 on the control plane, and restart its services.
2. Re-run the gateway grant.
3. Install both timers.
4. Confirm workers actually sleep and that the readiness check's maintenance failure clears.

That's a production change to `.172` and `.173`, so check you're OK with it before I start.

All prerequisites are in place:
- **Node (`.172`):**
  - the `maludb-gateway` user, `gateway.env` with the gateway's database URL, and the polkit rule;

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 2 is up as PR #206 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/206) and not merged. Slice 1 isn't deployed yet: #205 and #203 are still waiting on CI.

**What slice 2 fixes.** Two missing links meant customer Auth email couldn't have worked:
1. **No email settings.** A customer-created project never got any; only the operator command `cp-manage project email` created them.
2. **No hook on the worker.** The code that starts a project's Auth worker never set the email hook, so GoTrue accepted sign-ups and sent nothing, with no error anywhere.

**How it works now:**
- **First start:** the first time a project's Auth worker starts, it creates platform-default email settings with a hook secret. Later starts reuse that secret, and a customer's own custom-domain settings are left alone.
- **Hook wiring:** GoTrue is pointed at the control plane's internal hook and given that secret. The test signs a message the way GoTrue does and checks it verifies against the secret the hook route loads.
- **Production:** the gateway refuses to start Auth without email configured, so a missing setting shows up as an error rather than as confirmation emails that never arrive.
- **Readiness check:** a new "email" check fails production when the MaluMail key or sender address is missing.
- **Docs:** `DEPLOYMENT.md` §1.5b and the env example files cover both hosts.

211 tests pass across the email, Auth worker, gateway, readiness-check and account-recovery suites.

**One tradeoff to confirm:** the email hook's content, including a confirmation token, travels from the node to the control plane's internal listener over plain HTTP. That's on the private network, the same as the internal listener's other traffic. I noted it in the commit's security-review line.

**To make email live on the rehearsal (H-1), I need two things from you:**
- **The MaluMail key:** add `MALUMAIL_API=<key>` to `/etc/maludb/control-plane.env` and `/etc/maludb/provisioner.env` on `.173` (both root-only). Please don't paste it in chat.
- **The sender address:** on a domain verified in MaluMail, for example `noreply@maludb.org`.

I'll set the node's hook address and restart the services.

**Slice 3 decision needed:** `projects.auth_enabled` defaults to off, and nothing outside the tests ever turns it on. So as things stand no customer project can use Auth at all: the gateway won't serve `/auth/v1`. With Supabase, Auth simply works. My recommendation is to turn it on for every project; the worker still starts only on the first Auth request, so idle projects cost nothing extra. Is that OK?

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 4 (Storage) is ready as PR #208 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/208). It isn't merged or deployed. I ran the related tests (213 passed, 10 skipped because they need the storage test cluster) and ruff is clean.

**What was missing.** The Storage code already existed and passed its tests, but nothing set a node up for it in production:
- SeaweedFS (the object store) only ran from a test script, and its network address disappeared on reboot.
- Nothing created the storage worker's config file (`storage.env`) or its metadata database.
- The storage service had never started a container. Its unit hid `/run/user`, which rootless Podman needs, so it failed with `lstat /run/user/<uid>: permission denied`. I fixed the unit and tested it under systemd on the dev box: the container started, the memory limit was enforced, and stopping the service removed the container. I haven't run the real storage-api image under it yet, so the first full run will be on the node.

**What's in the PR**
- **An object store service.** SeaweedFS's internal parts (master, volume server, filer) check no credentials, so they listen only on the node's data address, 10.91.0.1. Only its S3 port also listens on the node's private address.
- **A firewall for it.** Before the store starts listening, its service loads a small nftables table of its own. Only the control plane can reach S3, and the gRPC port next to it, which has no authentication. Packets from other machines to the data address are dropped.
- **A data address that survives reboot**, set up through systemd-networkd.
- **`cp-manage node storage-prepare --name node-01 --print env|identities`.** It creates the node's storage secret and metadata database, then prints one of the two node config files. It refuses to print to a terminal, so the secrets only go through a pipe.
- **A preflight check, "object store".** It confirms the bucket answers from the control plane and every active node has been prepared.
- **Docs:** the runbook is DEPLOYMENT §2.7, plus ADR-085 and the env examples.

**Decision for you: ADR-085 (proposed).** The control plane itself measures stored bytes and deletes a removed project's files, so it has to reach the object store across the network. The ADR does that with the S3 port on the private network behind the firewall. The alternatives were running those jobs on the node (which would put the key-encryption key and control-plane write access there) or putting the store on the control plane.

I found and fixed one security problem in my own runbook: it first put the S3 key, which opens every customer's files, in `control-plane.env`. The internet-facing public app loads that file, so the key now goes in `provisioner.env` only.

**Known gaps (written up in the ADR and §2.7)**
- **One copy of each file.** A lost node disk loses customer files until the backup slice covers the store.
- **No automatic recovery.** One maintenance job (`storage_tenants`) needs to reach the storage worker on the node's own loopback, so it does nothing from the control plane. If the worker ever loses its tenant list, someone has to fix it by hand.
- **Realtime has the same unit bug.** It isn't a launch feature, so I left it for the leftovers slice.

**Waiting on you**
- **Merges:** #204 (plan), #206 (email), #207 (Auth on by default), #208 (Storage).
- **Go-ahead to deploy #208 to the rehearsal VMs.** On node 172 that means a firewall table, a new `maludb-objects` user, a subuid range and lingering for `maludb-api`, a `pg_hba` reload, and starting two new services. It also restarts the provisioner on 173 after the S3 settings are added. No PostgreSQL restart is needed, because the node already listens on `*`. I'll generate the S3 secret on the hosts, so it never appears in chat.
- **The MaluMail key** (H-1), which you place in the env files, still blocks deploying email.

## 🤖 Claude

Email is deployed and working on test.maludb.org: a signup through supabase-js on project `8zn07rbf` got its confirmation email handed to MaluMail. Getting there took a fix that's in PR #209, which isn't merged yet.

**What happened**
- **#206 merge:** it conflicted again after #208 merged. I rebased it (keeping both sides of each conflict) and CI went green. I merged it as a67af66 and deployed that to both VMs.
- **The bug:** after deploying, every Auth request on the beta answered 503. GoTrue refuses a plain-HTTP hook URL unless the host is loopback ("only localhost, 127.0.0.1, and ::1 are supported with http"). Slice 2 pointed the hook at the control plane's private address, and the tests had only ever used 127.0.0.1, so they never caught it. This came to light because #207 had just turned Auth on.
- **The fix, PR #209** (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/209):
  - A loopback-only relay on the node, using stock `systemd-socket-proxyd`, from `127.0.0.1:8119` to the control plane's internal listener. It only forwards traffic; the control plane still checks each hook's signature.
  - The gateway now refuses to start with a plain-HTTP hook URL that isn't loopback, so this fails at startup instead of on a customer's first Auth request.
  - A new test runs the real GoTrue binary and shows it rejects the old URL and accepts the relay. The related tests pass locally (117).
  - I haven't merged it, since you only authorised #206. I installed just the relay and the config change on node .172 straight from the branch. The node's code is still main.

**Checked on the beta**
- A supabase-js signup on `8zn07rbf` was accepted. The hook reached the control plane through the relay (200), and MaluMail accepted the send (200).
- I used your platform sender address as the recipient, so nothing went to a third-party inbox. Whether it landed in an inbox is on MaluMail's side and I didn't check that.
- I deleted the test user and revoked both temporary keys.

**Next**
- **Merge #209** once its CI is green. After that, pulling main on .172 lines the node's code up with what's already installed; no other deploy step is needed.
- **Still failing preflight:** the signup challenge (Turnstile keys, H-2). The maintenance pass also still exits 1 because node-01 has no backup yet (slice 7).

## 🤖 Claude

The memory services are installed on the rehearsal control plane (.173) and running as their own narrowed roles. The end-to-end test hasn't run yet: it needs your provider keys.

**Installed**
- **Egress proxy** on 127.0.0.1:3128. It only allows api.anthropic.com, api.openai.com and api.voyageai.com.
- **Memory worker**, logging in as `memworker`, a member of `cp_memory_worker`.
- **Query embedder** on 10.120.0.173:8114, logging in as `memembed`, a member of `cp_memory_embedder`.
- Both role passwords were generated on the host and written straight into `/etc/maludb/memory-worker.env` and `memory-embedder.env` (mode 600). I then ran `cp-manage memory-worker grant`.
- Node .172's `gateway.env` now points at the embedder.
- Preflight's "memory worker role" and "memory embedder role" checks, which were warnings, now pass.

**Two problems found, fixed in PR #210** (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/210, not merged):
1. **The embedder was reachable from the whole private network, including the dev box.** Gateways send customers' secret keys to that port. The unit now allows loopback only, and each deployment lists its nodes in a drop-in. I've already applied this on .173: the node gets 200 and the dev box is blocked.
2. **Preflight falsely failed "operator console role" once the memory roles existed.** `pg_has_role` is true for superusers on every role, so `postgres` and `maludb` looked like both console roles and memory workers. The check now ignores superusers. This one is code, so preflight will keep showing the failure on .173 until #210 is merged and deployed.

Tests pass locally (136).

**What I need from you:** in the console at test.maludb.org, open project `8zn07rbf`, then:
- set the Anthropic and Voyage keys under Memory → provider keys;
- create a memory space, for example `notes`.

Please enter the keys in the console rather than pasting them here. Tell me when they're in. I'll then send a text ingest through the gateway and check that the worker extracts and embeds it through the proxy. After that I'll check search by text through the embedder, and that a publishable key is refused. Your Voyage key allows only 3 requests a minute, so I'll run it once.

Back to slice 6. Pins and the grants were already done on node-01. The remaining part, a customer enabling the two features, needs a decision:
- **Customer route:** `POST /v1/projects/<ref>/maludb/datamodel/enable` and `.../vectors/enable`, which queue a job the provisioner runs. They need an **owner's personal access token**.
- **Operator path:** `cp-manage project maludb enable`, which I can run myself. It would only prove the node side, not what a customer actually does.
- **No console buttons:** the console has no way to turn either feature on, so a free customer can only use the API with a token. I'll list that as a gap for slice 9.

To test the customer path, please:
1. In the console, go to **Access tokens** (`https://test.maludb.org/#/tokens`), create a token named e.g. `slice6-check`, and give it a short expiry.
2. Save it on the dev box the same way as your provider keys, not in chat:
   ```
   (umask 077; cat > ~/maludb-provisioner-api/.dev/slice6-pat)   # paste, Enter, then Ctrl-D
   ```

- the graph tables and vector compartments work;

## 🤖 Claude

Before slice 7 goes further I need three decisions from you: where the off-site backups live (H-3), where the offline copy of the KEK is kept, and how backups run on the node. What would you like to clarify about them?

For context on each:

1. **Off-site repository.** pgBackRest can send an encrypted copy to an S3-compatible bucket (B2, R2, Wasabi, AWS), to another host over SFTP, or just to local disk for the beta.
2. **KEK copy.** A control-plane backup is useless without the KEK, so it needs to be kept somewhere apart from the backups: a password manager, offline media, or a separate account.
3. **Runner.** Today `cp-manage node backup` runs pgBackRest where the control-plane database is, but on our two machines that database isn't on the node.
   - My proposal is a timer on the node that runs pgBackRest as `postgres` and records each result through a role that can call one function for its own node. That's the pattern the health reporter already uses, and it keeps the KEK off the node.
   - The alternative is the control plane driving backups over SSH, which ADR-083 already rejected.

I haven't built anything for slice 7 yet. #213 is still waiting on CI; I'll merge it once it's green.

## 🧑 User

Is the S3 bucket free? looking for a free option for the storage backups?  I do have another proxmox server

## 🤖 Claude

Yes, there are free options, and at our current size (about 62 MB across the whole cluster) all of these fit easily. My pricing knowledge may be out of date, so check each provider's current page before signing up.

**Free S3-compatible buckets**
- **Cloudflare R2:** 10 GB storage free each month, with **no egress fees**, so restores cost nothing. Needs a Cloudflare account, which you already have for Turnstile. This is my pick.
- **Backblaze B2:** first 10 GB free. Downloads beyond a small daily allowance are billed.
- **AWS S3:** free tier is only 5 GB for the first 12 months, then it's paid. I'd skip it.
- **Oracle Cloud Always Free:** includes object storage, but signup is heavier and accounts sometimes get reclaimed.

Backups are encrypted by pgBackRest before they leave the node, so the provider only ever holds ciphertext.

**Your other Proxmox server**
It's free, and it's a very good second repository: restores are fast and don't depend on the internet. Whether it counts as *off-site* depends on where it is:
- **Different building or site:** it can be the off-site copy by itself, as an SFTP repository on a small VM there.
- **Same rack, room or power:** it protects against a failed host or disk, but not against losing the site (fire, power, theft). I'd still add R2 for that.

**My recommendation:** use both. pgBackRest can write to two repositories at once, so every backup and all the WAL would go to both:
- **repo1** on a VM on your other Proxmox server, for fast restores;
- **repo2** in Cloudflare R2's free tier, as the true off-site copy.

It costs nothing at this size. The node's own disk stops being the only copy, which is what ADR-064 requires.

Two questions:
1. Is the other Proxmox server at a different physical location from the one running .172/.173?
2. Is R2 alongside it fine with you, or do you want the Proxmox server only for now?

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 7 needs an architecture decision before any building, so I've written it up as ADR-086 in PR #214 (docs only): https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/214. It uses your two choices: a VM on your second Proxmox server at the other site, and Cloudflare R2's free tier.

**Why it isn't just configuration**
- **Where backups run:** `cp-manage node backup` runs pgBackRest where the control-plane database is. Neither of our machines has both pgBackRest with the data and write access to that database.
- **One repository only:** the backup code only understands a single repository, so two means code changes.
- **Node settings:** `archive_mode` is off on the node, and turning it on means restarting its PostgreSQL once.
- **Nothing else is copied:** nothing backs up the control plane, and customer files in SeaweedFS still exist as one copy.

**What ADR-086 proposes**
1. **Backups run on the node** on a timer, as `postgres`: a daily full backup plus differentials every 6 hours. Results are recorded through a role that can only call two functions for its own node, the same pattern as the health reporter. The node gets no KEK.
2. **Two repositories, neither on the node, both encrypted by pgBackRest.**
   - **repo1** is SFTP to the VM at the other site.
   - **repo2** is R2.
   - Every backup and all the WAL go to both, and each one is checked separately.
3. **`archive_mode = on`:** one restart of the node's PostgreSQL, scheduled with you, before signups open.
4. **The control plane** is dumped nightly, encrypted, and shipped to both destinations.
5. **Customer files** get a nightly `rclone sync` from SeaweedFS to a second R2 bucket.
6. **Restore** stays an attended command run on the node. That's a documented exception to ADR-083, which ruled out a standing process with control-plane access on the node, not a one-off run.
7. **Preflight** fails on a failed or stale backup check, instead of passing a node just because a stanza name is recorded (finding 21).

The plan now splits slice 7 into 7a–7e: the recording role, the node runner, the two repositories, the control plane plus files, and the restore test.

**What I need from you**
- **Accept or change ADR-086.** The open points are the node-side runner and recording role, the rclone copy of files, and the restore exception.
- **H-3 setup:**
  - **VM at the other site.** Can node .172 reach it over SSH (site-to-site VPN, or a public address with a port open)? Tell me how, and whether you want me to set up its backup account over SSH.
  - **R2.** Create two buckets (backups, files) and an API token scoped to each. You'll place the tokens on the node yourself; I'll give you the exact file and commands.
  - **KEK copy.** This is still unanswered: where the KEK and staff key live off both hosts. A password manager is the simple choice, as long as it holds neither backup credential.

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 7a is merged (#215, along with the ADR-086 docs in #214), and 7b is open as PR #216 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/216). Neither is deployed yet.

**7b: the node's backup runner**
- **`node_backup.py`** runs on the node as `postgres`, with no keys (a test checks it never loads the KEK code).
  - **`full` / `diff`:** writes a running row through the recorder role, runs pgBackRest with `--start-fast`, and closes the row with pgBackRest's own label and sizes. A failure is recorded with its reason. A backup that hangs leaves the row running so the maintenance pass reports it.
  - **`check`:** inspects the repositories on the node and records a report.
- **The node's report is treated as untrusted input.** A report that's missing, over 26 hours old, or for the wrong stanza counts as "not examined", never as healthy.
  - The node decides whether a repository shares its disk. Run from the control plane, that check would have looked at the control plane's own disks and answered about the wrong host.
  - `cp-manage node backup-check` now combines the node's report with the cluster settings it reads remotely.
- **Units:**
  - a service template running as `postgres` with a 6-hour limit;
  - timers: a full backup daily at 02:15, differentials at 08:15, 14:15 and 20:15, and checks every six hours.
- **Preflight, finding 21:** a node now needs a passing backup check under a week old and a repository report under 26 hours old. That's a failure in production. A recorded stanza name used to be enough.
- **Tests:** 19 new; 294 pass across the backup, recorder, preflight, units, restore and maintenance suites.

**Deploying 7a**
It adds migration 0058, and after it the gateway grant must be re-run on the control plane. Until then preflight's "gateway role" check fails. I'll deploy it together with 7b once #216 is merged, unless you want 7a on the VMs sooner.

**Next is 7c**, the two repositories plus `archive_mode = on`. It's blocked on your H-3 setup:
1. **VM at the other site:** how can node .172 reach it over SSH, via a site-to-site VPN or a public address and port? And do you want me to create its backup account over SSH?
2. **R2:** two buckets, one for backups and one for files, each with an API token limited to that bucket. You'll place the tokens on the node yourself.
3. **KEK copy:** where the KEK and staff key will live, away from both hosts.
4. **Restart window:** turning on `archive_mode` restarts the node's PostgreSQL once. Tell me when that's acceptable.

- The node's env file got the password straight from the control plane over SSH; it was never displayed, and the temporary copy was shredded.

**Node (.172)**
- `/etc/maludb/node-backup.env` is installed (mode 600, root), along with the backup service and its three timers.
- **Only the check timer is enabled**; the next check runs at 21:45 UTC. Full and differential backups stay off until 7c sets up the repositories, because running them now would only record failures.
- I ran one check by hand. It recorded that pgBackRest answers but `check` fails, and that the repository sits on the node's own disk.
- The gateway, node maintenance and health reporter are all still running.

**What the control plane now reports** (`node backup-check`, exit 1):
- `archive_mode` is off (turning it on needs a PostgreSQL restart);
- pgBackRest has no stanza configured;
- retention is unset;
- the repository is on the same disk as the data, which is a production failure under ADR-064.

**Preflight:** "node backups" now **fails** for these reasons, where it used to pass because a stanza name was recorded (finding 21). It stays failing until 7c is deployed and checked.

**7c is blocked on you:**
1. **VM at the other site:** how node .172 reaches it over SSH (VPN, or a public address and port), and whether I should create the backup account on it.

Restart window noted, so I'll do the node work now. One constraint shapes the order: `archive_mode = on` needs a **working** `archive_command`. With archiving on and nothing archiving, PostgreSQL keeps every WAL segment until the node's disk fills. The off-site repositories are still waiting on your H-3 setup, so the plan is:

1. **Interim local repository** (`/var/lib/pgbackrest` on the node), encrypted, with 14-day retention. It makes archiving and backups real today, but it doesn't meet ADR-086, and preflight will keep failing it as co-located, which is correct. It gets replaced once the other-site VM and R2 exist.
2. **`archive_mode = on`**, then restart the node's PostgreSQL once, create the stanza, run `pgbackrest check`, take the first full backup through the runner, and turn on the full and diff timers.
3. **Code for 7c:** readiness that understands several repositories (repo1, repo2), so the off-site repositories slot in without further code.

## 🤖 Claude

node-01 is now actually backed up: WAL archiving is on and the first full backup is taken and recorded. For now the backups sit in an interim repository on the node's own disk. The 7c code for the two off-site repositories is in PR #217 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/217), not merged.

**What I did on node-01**
- **pgBackRest config:** I rewrote `/etc/pgbackrest.conf` (the old template is kept as `.orig`). It sets up the interim local repository, encrypted with a passphrase generated on the node, keeping 30 days.
- **Why a local repository first:** with `archive_mode` on, PostgreSQL keeps every WAL file until it's archived. Turning archiving on without a working destination would slowly fill the disk, and the off-site destinations are still waiting on your setup.
- **Archiving:** `archive_mode = on` with a 5-minute `archive_timeout`. PostgreSQL restarted once at 16:31 UTC and came straight back; the gateway, storage and reporter stayed up.
- **First backup:** the stanza is created and `pgbackrest check` passes. The full backup `20260917-163138F` shrank 63 MB to 6.4 MB in 20 seconds, and it was recorded through the recorder role.
- **Timers:** full, diff and check are all on. The next diff is at 20:15 UTC and the next full at 02:15.
- **Retention finding:** I first set 14 days. The check correctly refused it because the longest plan promises 30, so it's now 30.

**Status**
- `cp-manage node backups` shows node-01 **ok**.
- `backup-check` fails only because the repository is on the same disk as the data (ADR-064). That's correct until the off-site repositories replace it.
- The plan records the interim repository as a temporary exception to ADR-086.

**PR #217 (7c code)**
- **Every repository checked separately:** readiness reads repo1–repo4 (type, path, retention) and never the keys or passphrases. It checks retention, the 30-day promise and co-location for each one.
- **Bug fixed along the way:** co-location was judged by path alone. An SFTP repository's path is a path on the *other site's* machine, but it would have been checked against the node's own disks. S3 and SFTP repositories are now off-host by definition.
- **Production warning:** it warns while fewer than two repositories are off the host.
- **Runbook:** `deploy/pgbackrest.conf.example` sets repo1 to SFTP at the other site (pinned host fingerprint) and repo2 to R2, both encrypted, 30 days. A test checks the example passes production readiness as written, and DEPLOYMENT §2.8 has the step-by-step.
- **Tests:** 195 passed across the backup, recorder, runner, preflight and restore suites.

**To replace the interim repository, I need from you (H-3):**
1. **VM at the other site:** how node .172 reaches it over SSH, and whether I should create the `pgbackrest` account there.
2. **R2:** two buckets (backups, files) and a token limited to each. You place the tokens on the node yourself.
3. **Offline copy:** where the KEK, the staff key and the pgBackRest passphrases will live. The interim repository's passphrase exists only on the node right now.

Merging #217 is safe now: it's code and docs, and it changes nothing about how the node runs today. Slices 7d (control-plane dump and a copy of customer files to R2) and 7e (restore drills) come after H-3.

## 🤖 Claude

Understood. The node's backups stay in the local repository for now, and the VM and R2 are off the list. Before I change anything, two consequences you should know, because the checks were built to refuse exactly this:

1. **Preflight will fail until something changes.** In production, ADR-064 treats a repository on the same disk as the data as "not a backup". So "node backups" stays a hard FAIL, and slice 10's launch check needs no failures. Leaving it failing would train everyone to ignore preflight. Weakening the check silently would be worse.
2. **The risk is real, even though it's cheap to accept for a beta.** One lost disk or VM takes the database *and* its backups. Customer files (SeaweedFS) also have a single copy, and the control plane has no dump yet.

My proposal is to record this as an explicit, time-limited exception rather than bend the check. Drafting it as ADR-087.

## 🤖 Claude

Done. The VM and R2 are off the list, and node-01 keeps backing up locally: WAL archiving, a nightly full backup, differentials every six hours, 30 days kept, and encrypted. That covers the likeliest beta problems, like a customer's mistake, a bad migration or a corrupted table, through point-in-time restore. It does **not** cover losing the node's disk or VM, which takes the database and its backups together.

**The catch:** the production backup check treats a repository on the same disk as "not a backup" (ADR-064). So preflight's "node backups" would stay a hard FAIL forever and block the launch check.

**ADR-087, proposed in PR #218** (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/218), records your decision as an explicit exception rather than weakening the check:
- **A per-node acceptance** you run with `cp-manage node backup-accept-local --name node-01 --until <date> --reason <text>`. While it's in force, only the same-disk failure becomes a warning naming who accepted it, why, and until when. Archiving, retention, `pgbackrest check` and the 30-day window still fail if they break.
- **At most 90 days,** and it lapses by itself; renewing means recording a new decision.
- **Preflight shows the acceptance,** so anyone reading it knows host-loss recovery isn't available.
- The launch plan records that the off-site targets are deferred (H-3).

**Still to know**
- **Customer files** in SeaweedFS remain a single copy.
- **Slice 9** (the public site and terms) must not promise backups beyond this.
- **PR #217** (the 7c code for multiple repositories) is still worth merging. It also makes the local repository check precise, and the off-site setup can be added later without code changes.

**Next, once you accept ADR-087:**
1. Build the acceptance command and run it for node-01, so preflight passes with the named warning.
2. **7d:** a nightly control-plane dump on the control-plane VM. It's a different VM from the node, so losing one doesn't take both.
3. **7e:** a point-in-time restore drill of one free project from the local repository, which is what proves the backups actually work.

Say "accept ADR-087" (and whether to merge #217 and #218), and I'll build it.

## 🤖 Claude

ADR-087 is accepted and #217 is merged. #218 conflicted with #217 in the plan file, so I rebased it; it merges automatically when its CI is green. The build is done and waiting in PR #219 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/219), which isn't merged or deployed yet.

**PR #219, commit 1: accepting local backups for the beta (ADR-087)**
- **The command:** `cp-manage node backup-accept-local --name node-01 --until <date> --reason <text>` records who accepted, why and until when; `--revoke` withdraws it.
- **Limits:** it refuses a past date, more than 90 days, or no reason. The database enforces the 90-day limit and "all fields or none" itself, so even a direct write can't stretch it.
- **What it changes:** only the "backups on the same disk" failure becomes a warning. Archiving, retention, `pgbackrest check` and the 30-day window still fail as before.
- **Preflight** names the acceptance while it's in force, and fails again **the day it lapses** without waiting for the next backup check.

**PR #219, commit 2: 7d, the control plane's nightly dump**
- **The command:** `cp-manage control-plane backup --dir <dir> --keep-days 14` writes a timestamped dump with owner-only permissions.
- **Pruning:** older dumps are deleted only after a good new dump, and never the last one, so a run of failed nights doesn't wipe the history.
- **Units:** a service and nightly timer that run as the provisioner user and write only `/var/backups/maludb-control-plane`.
- **Preflight** fails in production when no dump is under 26 hours old.
- **Runbook:** DEPLOYMENT now says to copy the KEK and staff key off the host by hand. A dump can't be restored without them.

**Tests:** 12 new, plus updated preflight and unit tests. 204 and 129 passed across the affected suites, and lint is clean.

**Once you approve merging #219, I'll deploy it:**
1. migration 0059 on the control plane;
2. `node backup-accept-local` for node-01 (I'd use 90 days, until 2026-12-16, with the reason "free-tier beta; off-host targets deferred");
3. `node backup-check`, so preflight's "node backups" passes with the acceptance named;
4. install the dump directory and timer on .173 and take the first dump now.

**7e, the restore drill, needs a yes from you first.** The restore command has to run on the node while writing to the control-plane database. On two machines that means temporarily giving the node:
- the provisioner's database login, with a `pg_hba` line admitting it;
- the KEK.

I'd take one free project back to a point in time **beside** its live database, leaving the live one untouched. Then I'd remove the credentials and the `pg_hba` line straight away. Say "merge 219, deploy, and run the drill" to do all three.

The fix is a few lines in `restore.py`.
