## 🤖 Claude

Here's where things stand.

**How much is left for the free tier:** engineering is about three-quarters done. Slices 1–7 are built, deployed and verified on test.maludb.org. What remains is slice 8 (in progress), slice 9 (mostly your text), and slice 10 (cleanup plus a real signup walkthrough) — roughly 2–3 more sessions like today. The bigger dependency is you: terms/privacy/AUP (H-4), an abuse reviewer (H-5), a support address (H-6), and copying the KEK off the host.

**#220** (the scratch-marker fix) is still in CI. Its watcher merges it automatically when green.

**Slice 8 started, and the bug is confirmed on the real deployment.** The chain is visitor → Nginx Proxy Manager (TLS) → Apache → uvicorn, so:
- Apache put **NPM's** address in the last `X-Forwarded-For` hop, and the code trusted the last hop;
- uvicorn also rewrote the client address on its own, because Apache connects from loopback;
- so every visitor was recorded as `10.120.0.1`. Signup and sign-in limits treated the entire internet as one client, and sessions and audit rows logged the proxy as everyone's address.

**The fix is in PR #221** (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/221), not merged:
- **`MALUDB_TRUSTED_PROXIES`** names the proxies in front. The client is the rightmost forwarded address that isn't one of them. Anything a caller forges sits to the left of what a proxy actually observed, so it can never be picked.
- **A range covering the internet is refused** outright, since trusting everyone would let each caller choose its own bucket.
- **`--no-proxy-headers`** on the three control-plane units stops uvicorn substituting its own guess.
- **Sessions, audit rows and the captcha check** now use the same address as the limits.
- **Tests:** 232 passed, including the two-proxy topology, forged headers, direct connections that bypass the proxies, and the config refusals.

**To finish slice 8 I need you to merge #221 so I can deploy it.** Then I'll set `MALUDB_TRUSTED_PROXIES=127.0.0.1,10.120.0.1` on .173 and prove it from outside: two different clients get separate buckets, and a forged header can't move one caller into another's.

The other half of slice 8 is **H-5**: who reviews the abuse report, and how often.

## 🤖 Claude

**The fix works.** Same test, after deploy:

| Client | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Dev box, 22 attempts | 20 × 401 then 429 | 20 × 401 then 429 (unchanged, correct) |
| Dev box with forged `X-Forwarded-For` | — | **429**: it can't claim a fresh bucket |
| Node .172 (different client) | **429** on its first attempt | **401**: its own bucket |
| Control plane .173 (third client) | — | **401**: its own bucket |

So the limits now count per visitor, a forged header can't move a caller out of its bucket, and one abuser no longer locks out everyone else. Recording the result and closing finding 19 in the plans.

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 8's engineering half is done, deployed and measured on the live site.

**Result** (`https://test.maludb.org`, same test before and after)

| Client | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Dev box, 22 sign-in attempts | 20 × 401, then 429 | 20 × 401, then 429 |
| Dev box with forged `X-Forwarded-For` | — | **429** — can't claim a fresh bucket |
| Node .172 | **429 on its first attempt** | **401** — its own bucket |
| Control plane .173 | — | **401** — its own bucket |

Before the fix, one client exhausting the limit locked out everyone, because all traffic was counted as the TLS proxy. Now each visitor is counted separately, and the same address is recorded in sessions and audit rows.

All three are done and waiting in two PRs.

**PR #223 — console buttons and the abuse cadence** (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/223)
- **A MaluDB page on every project.** For the schema graph and vector compartments it shows whether each is on, the plan's limits, and the latest job, with buttons to turn them on or off (plus **Refresh now** for the graph). That closes the gap you hit in slice 6, where a personal access token was the only way in.
- **It follows the work.** Enabling is queued and runs on the project's host, so the page tracks the job rather than guessing.
- **It offers no reader**, because both features are read with the project's **secret key**, which a browser session isn't. The page shows an example instead.
- **Only owners and admins** see the buttons, matching the API.
- **Docs:** `docs.html` gains the section the page links to; there was nothing on either feature before.
- **H-5 recorded:** the console's Abuse review page and DEPLOYMENT §5 both state that **the owner reviews it weekly**.
- I rendered the page to check the layout. 78 tests pass.

**PR #224 — legal drafts** (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/224)
- **Terms, Privacy and Acceptable Use**, written from what the platform actually does, linked from both footers and from the signup form.
- **They state the beta's real limits** rather than a template's promises: one machine, backups kept beside the data, a single copy of uploaded files, and restore to the most recent backup for free projects.
- **Privacy lists only the processors the code calls** (MaluMail, Cloudflare Turnstile, the host), and says memory providers are called with *your customers' own keys*, under their own agreements.
- **Acceptable use** describes enforcement as it works: automatic limits, the weekly report, and suspension as a human decision.
- **Tests** hold those claims and keep every operator blank visible as a `[PLACEHOLDER]`.

**Two warnings about #224**
1. **I'm not a lawyer and nobody has reviewed this.** Treat it as a first draft for a professional to check, especially the liability cap and governing law.
2. **It won't launch as-is.** Every `[PLACEHOLDER]` needs filling: legal entity, registered address, jurisdiction, retention periods, the liability amount, and the support, security and abuse addresses (H-6).

**Still open for the free tier:** merge these two, fill the legal placeholders, give me H-6's addresses, then slice 10 (remove the old install's services, the leftover `pg_hba` lines, finding 11, DEPLOYMENT §5, and a real signup walkthrough) and finally flipping signups on.

**One thing I won't touch without you**

Both VMs carry this line in `pg_hba.conf`:

```
host all all 10.120.0.250/32 scram-sha-256
```

That's **any role, into any database, with TLS not required**, from 10.120.0.250. That host answers ping, but nothing in the repo or the runbook names it, and it isn't this dev box (which is .165). It's left over from the previous install.

**What is 10.120.0.250?** Then I'll either remove the line, or narrow it to a named role over TLS and document why it exists.

## 🤖 Claude

The walkthrough worked, and it found one launch blocker.

**What passed**, as a stranger would do it:
- **The real form:** the page, legal links and footer render, and **Turnstile refused to submit without a solved challenge**. Cloudflare blocked my automated browser, which is the widget doing its job — so a human still needs to do one browser signup by hand.
- **The rest, through the API** with the challenge briefly disabled: signup (201), sign-in, a personal organization, and a project that reached PROVISIONED (serving).
- **The project works:** publishable and secret keys issued, a table created through the SQL editor, and with the official Supabase client the secret key reads and writes, project Auth accepts a signup, and Storage creates a bucket, uploads and downloads.
- **Restored afterwards:** the challenge is required again, signups are closed again, and an unchallenged signup is refused (400). I revoked the walkthrough keys.

**Expected, not a fault:** a new table has no row-level security, so the publishable key can read it — same as Supabase. The docs say to turn RLS on, and the table browser warns about that exact table. My test asserted the wrong thing.

**The blocker: nothing can delete a project.** There's no customer route, no `cp-manage` command, and `project cleanup` refuses any database that was ever provisioned. That matters three ways:
1. **The legal pages I drafted promise deletion** ("delete a project or your account at any time", and deletion on request in Privacy). Today that's untrue.
2. **A beta stores personal data**, so honouring a deletion request needs to be possible.
3. **Right now it's visible:** the walkthrough's project `3u2rzs6e` and its account can't be removed, and they hold a project slot on the node.

I've written it up as **slice 10b**. Most of the machinery exists — `jobs.cleanup` already drops roles, the database and the project's objects for failed projects — so the work is a guarded path for a live project: a customer route that marks it and queues the work, an operator command, the storage tenant deregistered, keys revoked, and the record kept.

**Also worth noting:** provisioning took **208 seconds**, where the docs and console copy say "about a minute". Either the copy or the timing should change.

**Your call:** shall I build slice 10b now? The alternative is to change the Terms and Privacy pages to promise deletion by request, which I'd rather not do while nobody can actually perform it.

## 🤖 Claude

Design for 10b, reusing the existing machinery rather than a second destroy path:
1. **Migration:** add `DELETING`/`DELETED` statuses and a `delete_requested_at` column.
2. **`jobs.delete_project`:** deregisters the storage tenant, removes objects, drops the database and roles, revokes keys, then records the project as deleted (row kept, as the audit trail).
3. **Provisioner:** claims projects in `DELETING` and runs the work, like it does provisioning.
4. **Customer route** `DELETE /v1/projects/{ref}` (owner/admin) and an operator command with an explicit confirmation.
5. **Console:** a delete control that makes you type the ref.

## 🤖 Claude

Slice 10b is built and in PR #226 (https://github.com/Mestopholis/maludb-provisioner-api/pull/226), rebased and mergeable. It merges automatically when CI is green, and I'll deploy it after that.

**What it does**
- **Asking to delete answers immediately:** the project stops serving and **every API key is revoked**, so a key already in someone's app stops working at once rather than whenever a worker gets to it.
- **The destruction runs on the node** in the provisioner: the storage registration, the database, the files in the bucket, then the per-tenant roles. That's the same order `cleanup` uses, for the same reasons.
- **The record survives.** The row stays with `deleted_at` set, so the project reference is never handed out again and the audit trail outlives the data.
- **Three ways in:** the API (owner or admin; a stranger gets a 404 so project existence isn't confirmed), `cp-manage project delete` which makes you name the project twice, and a console card that makes you type the reference.
- **Guards:** it refuses anything without a recorded request, a database whose name doesn't match the project's reference, and a project with provisioning still in flight.

**One thing the tests caught:** my first version imported node-side machinery into the public API, which breaks ADR-038. The request half now lives in `models` and only the destructive half is in `jobs`.

**Also handled**
- **#222** — you were right, it never merged and had conflicts in both plan files. Rebased, resolved keeping every true entry, merging when green.
- **#225** merged and deployed to both VMs: the first-key race fix and the rewritten DEPLOYMENT §5.

**Still needed from you before signups open**
1. Fill the legal placeholders and have a lawyer read them (H-4), plus the support, security and abuse addresses (H-6).
2. One browser signup by hand, since Cloudflare blocks my automated browser.
3. Then I flip `MALUDB_SIGNUPS_OPEN` to true as the last change.

I'd also like to delete the walkthrough project `3u2rzs6e` once #226 is deployed, as the first real use of the new path.

| Placeholder | Suggested |

**3. Numbers I can fill from what the platform really does — just confirm**

| Placeholder | Proposed | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `[DATE]` ×3 | the day you publish | |
| `[RETENTION]` ×2 | 30 days | node backups keep 30 days |
| `[LOG RETENTION]` | 30 days | matches journald's rotation on the VMs |
| `[RESPONSE TIME]` (privacy) | 30 days | standard for a data request |
| `[RESPONSE TIME]` (abuse) | 2 business days | |

| Placeholder | Note |
|---|---|

**5. The one I won't guess:** `[AMOUNT]`, the liability cap in the terms. I drafted "50 USD" as a placeholder. On a free tier the cap is usually small or nominal, but the number and whether it's enforceable where you are is a lawyer's call.

**Fastest path:** give me answers for groups 1, 2 and 4, plus a yes to group 3's defaults and a number for the cap. I'll fill them all and put it in a PR for your lawyer to read as finished text rather than a template.

## 🤖 Claude

#226 is deployed to both VMs (main 7d52e49, migration 0060 applied), and I used it for real: **the walkthrough project is gone** — database dropped, 6 roles removed, its uploaded file deleted from the bucket, and the record kept as DELETED so the reference is never reissued.

**Preflight now exits 2 with no failures.** The only two warnings are artefacts of running it with the provisioner's environment: it can't check the gateway role from the control plane, and it doesn't see the console's bind setting in that file.

**One finding from using it, and it must be fixed before signups open.** Deleting a project **couldn't deregister the shared storage worker**, because that worker's admin API listens on the node's loopback and the control plane can't reach it — the gap ADR-085 already noted. What it leaves behind is the deleted tenant's **database URL and JWT secret** in the worker's metadata, pointing at a database that no longer exists. I removed this one by hand on the node, but every future deletion would leave one.

I've recorded it as **slice 10c** in PR #227 (docs only), with the two options:
- a node-side reconciliation of the worker's tenant list, which keeps that admin port off the network as ADR-085 intended;
- or admitting the control plane to that port, the way ADR-085 already admits it to S3.

I'd build the first. Note for whoever does: deletion clears `node_id`, so the node's own role can't see the deleted row afterwards — the instruction has to be recorded rather than inferred.

**Where the free tier stands**
- **Done:** slices 1–10b, all deployed and verified on test.maludb.org.
- **Mine:** slice 10c.
- **Yours:** the legal answers I emailed to jlehman@kineticseas.com, the support/security/abuse addresses, and one browser signup by hand.
- **Last step, when you say so:** flipping signups on.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - `services/control_plane/storage_workers.py` — slice 4 added `prepare_node()`, `render_identities()`; 10c (in progress) added `known_tenants`:
     ```python
     def known_tenants(*, admin_port: int, api_key: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:
         listing = _admin("GET", "/tenants", admin_port=admin_port, api_key=api_key)
         if not isinstance(listing, list):
             raise StorageWorkerError("the worker's tenant listing was not a list")
         return tuple(
             item["id"] for item in listing
             if isinstance(item, dict) and isinstance(item.get("id"), str)
             and models.is_valid_project_ref(item["id"])
         )
     ```
     `_admin` returns `response.json()` (so a list body passes through), `None` on 404 with `expect_missing_ok`, `{}` on empty/unparseable, raises `StorageWorkerError` on >=400.
   - `deploy/maludb-object-store.service`, `deploy/10-maludb-data.{netdev,network}`, `deploy/object-store-firewall.nft`, `deploy/object-store.env.example` — SeaweedFS with master/volume/filer on 10.91.0.1 and S3 only on node private address; nft table admits only the control plane to 8333/18333 and drops off-host traffic to the data address.

Design settled: **no migration needed** — a node-side reconciler connects as the gateway role, whose RLS (migration 0031 `gateway_own_node` policies + `public.gateway_node_id()`) shows only live projects of its own node; refs the worker holds that are not visible (deleted, or moved to another node) are deregistered. The admin API key comes from `/etc/maludb/storage/storage.env` (`SERVER_ADMIN_API_KEYS`, `MALUDB_STORAGE_ADMIN_HOST_PORT`), so **no KEK is needed**; the gateway DSN comes from `/etc/maludb/gateway.env`. ## 🤖 Claude

Both merged and built:

**#227 merged** — the plan entry recording the storage-deregistration gap.

**Slice 10c built, PR #228 open** — a deleted tenant now leaves nothing on the node.

The shape of it: a hourly node-side pass (`node_storage reconcile`) running as `maludb-gateway`. It asks the worker what tenants it holds, asks the control plane which of those this node still serves, and deregisters the rest. No new database role, no migration, no opening of the admin port.

Two things worth flagging:

**The plan's worry was actually the mechanism.** The entry said that because `delete_project` clears `node_id`, the gateway can no longer see the deleted row — so the deregistration would have to be *recorded* as work. It's the opposite: ADR-072's policies key on `node_id`, so clearing it makes the project invisible to exactly the role doing the reconciling, and "the worker holds a ref this node can't see" already means "remove it." That also covers a project moved to another node, which a queued instruction wouldn't have.

**I fixed one finding in my own draft before committing.** The obvious unit took the admin credential from the worker's `storage.env` via `EnvironmentFile`. That file also holds `AUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` and the multitenant database URL — between them every tenant's Storage credentials on the node — plus the object store's secret key. A process environment is readable by any process of the same user, so that would have handed all of it to `maludb-gateway` once an hour to save writing a file. Narrowed to a two-value `reconcile.env`, rendered by the same `storage-prepare` run so the two can't drift.

Also split the admin API into `storage_admin`, a leaf importing only `httpx` and `models`, so this node-side pass can't reach the key ring — `node_maintenance`'s rule, asserted by an import-closure walk. All names re-exported; no caller changed.

It fails closed throughout: an unreachable control plane or an unreadable listing deregisters nothing, and more than 50 stale refs in one pass is refused and named, since that's the shape of a gateway role that lost its node mapping.

22 new tests pass locally, including the RLS assertions against a real gateway role. Full suite and CI are running; I'll merge #228 when it's green — say the word and I'll deploy it to the rehearsal node, where the by-hand deregistration from the 10b drill means there should be nothing left for the first pass to find.

One local note: your test database needed two fixes to run the gateway-role tests at all — four tables were owned by `cp_ci` rather than `cp_dev`, and `cp_dev` lacked `CREATEROLE` plus `createrole_self_grant = 'set'`. Those tests were silently skipping before. I changed the dev cluster, not the repo.

## 🤖 Claude

Status on the two things you asked for:

**#227 — merged.** Confirmed on `origin/main`.

**10c — built, PR #228 open**, CI running.

The reconciler runs hourly on the node as `maludb-gateway`: it asks the storage worker what tenants it holds, asks the control plane which of those the node still serves, and deregisters the rest. No new role, no migration, and the admin port stays off the network.

The plan entry predicted this would need the deregistration *recorded* as work, because `delete_project` clears `node_id` and hides the row from the gateway. That turned out to be the mechanism rather than the obstacle — ADR-072's policies key on `node_id`, so a ref the worker holds that the gateway cannot see is definitionally one this node shouldn't serve. It also picks up projects moved to another node, which a queued instruction wouldn't.

I caught and fixed one issue in my own first draft: the unit read the worker's `storage.env`, which carries `AUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, the multitenant DB URL and the object store's secret — every tenant's Storage credentials on that node — and a process environment is readable by any process of the same user. Narrowed to a two-value `reconcile.env` rendered by the same `storage-prepare` command. Also split the admin API into a leaf module so node-side code can't reach the key ring, asserted by an import-closure walk.

**On the local suite:** 1774 passed, but 3 failed and 6 errored. The errors reproduce identically on unmodified `main` — they're tests that were silently *skipping* until I granted `cp_dev` CREATEROLE, and now fail because `MALUDB_NODE_ADMIN_DSN` is unset on this box. I'm re-running with full output to identify the 3 failures rather than assume they're the same category; I'll confirm against `main` before claiming they're unrelated, and won't ask you to merge #228 until I have.

Two things for you when convenient:

- Your test database had four tables owned by `cp_ci` instead of `cp_dev`, and `cp_dev` lacked CREATEROLE — so the gateway-role RLS tests have been skipping locally. I fixed the cluster, not the repo. Setting `MALUDB_NODE_ADMIN_DSN` against a disposable cluster would un-skip the rest, but that creates and drops `mldb_*` roles and databases, so I've left it for you to decide.
- Deploy of 10c to the rehearsal node needs your go-ahead. The by-hand deregistration you did during the 10b drill means the first pass should find nothing — a clean way to see it report `0 tenant(s) deregistered` before it ever has real work.

Merge watcher is armed on #228 — it only merges if every check is SUCCESS. Deploy scripts are staged. Three things still outstanding before anything lands:

**CI `check` on #228** — still pending (`security-review` passed).

**Local full suite** — at ~42%. This is the run that will finally name the 3 failures the earlier truncated run reported. I want those identified before the deploy, not after.

**Both hosts verified clean** at 7d52e49, working trees unmodified — so a fast-forward is safe on each.

The deploy, once #228 merges:

1. `.173`: fast-forward `/opt/maludb`. No listener restart — 10c adds no route and no control-plane behaviour, only the node-side pass and the command that renders its env file.
2. `.173` → `.172`: `storage-prepare --print reconcile` piped straight to `/etc/maludb/storage/reconcile.env`, root 0600, the same host-to-host pipe the other node credentials use — it never touches a terminal.
3. `.172`: fast-forward, install the unit and timer, `daemon-reload`, enable.
4. Run it once by hand rather than waiting for the hour, and read the journal.

On the node the expected first result is `reconcile finished: 0 tenant(s) deregistered` — you removed `3u2rzs6e`'s registration by hand during the 10b drill, so there should be nothing left. If it reports more than zero, that's interesting rather than alarming: it would mean an earlier deletion left something the drill didn't catch, which is exactly the class of thing this pass exists to sweep up. I'll report the number either way rather than assuming zero.

If the local suite turns up a real regression from my change, I'll stop the merge before it happens and tell you — the watcher merges only on all-green CI, and CI has the fuller environment, so a genuine break should fail there too.


Both fail with `FileNotFoundError: No such file or directory: 'gotrue'` — the GoTrue binary isn't installed on this box and `MALUDB_GOTRUE_BIN` is unset. I ran them against unmodified `main` in the main checkout: identical failure, so they predate slice 10c.

Worth noting as a small defect in its own right, though: that test *fails* where it should *skip*. Every other binary-dependent test in the suite skips and contributes a line to the "security properties not verified" banner. This one raises `FileNotFoundError` out of the `subprocess` call instead, so a missing GoTrue reads as a broken build rather than an unverified property. That's the failure mode AGENTS.md is emphatic about getting right, just inverted — noisy rather than silent. Cheap to fix with the same guard the others use; say the word and I'll do it as a separate change rather than smuggling it into 10c.

The third failure is still ahead — the run is at 2248 of 2399 tests, and the two above plus the five `test_extension_upgrade` errors are all that's appeared so far. The remaining ~150 tests include `test_storage_*` and `test_node_storage`, so if 10c did break something, that's where it will show. I'll have the name in a few minutes.

To be clear about what this means for the merge: the watcher on #228 only fires on all-green CI, and CI has GoTrue, the node DSN, and the rest of the environment this box lacks — so none of these three would fail there. If CI goes red, I'll stop and report rather than merge.

## 🤖 Claude

The local suite finished, and the answer changed: **there were only ever 2 real failures.**

```
2 failed, 1774 passed, 618 skipped, 5 errors in 614s
```

Both are the GoTrue ones I already traced to a missing binary, reproduced on unmodified `main`. The five `test_extension_upgrade` errors are the same category — they need `MALUDB_NODE_ADMIN_DSN`, which this box doesn't have.

The third failure from the first run was **my own fault, not the code's**. I had started that full suite in the background and then kept running targeted suites against the same test database while it was going. Two pytest processes truncating tables and creating roles concurrently deadlocked on `pg_class` — I saw that deadlock in a targeted run and stopped the background suite, but the damage was already recorded in its count. A clean serial run shows 2. Nothing from slice 10c.

So the local picture is clean. Worth my remembering not to overlap runs against one test database — the failure it produces looks like a real bug and costs a ten-minute re-run to disprove.

CI `check` on #228 is still pending; the merge watcher is armed and will only fire on all-green. Deploy scripts are staged and both hosts are clean at 7d52e49. I'll report the merge and then the deploy result — including whatever number the first reconcile pass actually prints, rather than the zero I expect.

## 🤖 Claude

**#228 merged (`bb08ce3`) and deployed. Verified working, not just installed.**

Both hosts fast-forwarded, `reconcile.env` piped host-to-host into `/etc/maludb/storage/reconcile.env` (root 0600, exactly two keys, never through a terminal), unit and timer installed and armed — next run 00:13 UTC, hourly after that.

The proof, in three passes:

1. **First pass:** `every registered tenant is a project this node serves (1)` → 0 deregistered. Correct, and it also settles the earlier confusion — `GET /tenants` answered 200, so my original 404 really was a bad probe hitting port 80.
2. **Planted a departed tenant** (`zzzztest`, a ref no project owns, dummy DSN, unreachable through the gateway): present 200 → pass runs → **404**, journal says `deregistered zzzztest: this node no longer serves it`, `1 tenant(s) deregistered`.
3. **Ran it again:** back to 0, live tenant untouched.

I did this with a planted registration rather than by provisioning and deleting a real project — same code path, no 208-second provision and no destruction of anything real.

**One thing the test surfaced that's worth your judgement.** My first attempt used `zzzz0test` — nine characters. The pass silently ignored it, because `known_tenants` drops anything that isn't a valid 8-character project ref before it can reach a URL path. That filter is right (the listing comes out of the worker's own database and AGENTS.md treats refs as untrusted), but the consequence is that a malformed registration is invisible to the reconciler *forever* — it can never be swept up, only removed by hand, which is what I did to clean up.

Nothing can currently create such a registration: `register_tenant` validates the ref on the way in, so the only source would be direct admin-API access or a corrupted metadata database. So it's a latent gap rather than a live one. The honest options are to leave it (and note it), or to have the pass count and log refs it had to skip so they're at least visible. I'd lean to the latter — one log line, no new behaviour — but it's a separate change and I'd rather ask than fold it into a merged slice.

Free-tier remaining: H-4 legal placeholders and H-6 addresses (both yours), one manual browser signup, then flipping `MALUDB_SIGNUPS_OPEN`.
